## Pass 328 result: Comoros, Djibouti and South Sudan issuance-taxonomy audit

Three exact, independently reviewed destination audits partition 90 unsupported relationships—30 each into Comoros, Djibouti and South Sudan—but add no normalizable current relationship. Complete-matrix evidence remains 40,012 of 44,974 relationships (89.0%), with 4,962 not covered and zero old or stale rows as of 25 August 2026. No regional total, displayed access label, mobility score or rank changes. Canonical evidence now contains 817 reviewed batches, 3,408 official source records and 1,940 policies.

Comoros' current Ministry of Justice publication of Law 88-025 requires a Comorian visa for foreigners subject to treaty and special-law exceptions. Crucially, its airport-or-port issuance language applies only to a transit visa of at most five days. The law separately defines a tourist visa of at most 45 days without naming its issuance point and authorizes Interior or delegated diplomatic and consular authorities to issue visas. Current Dutch and Slovenian guidance establishes visa liability and mission referral only; Singapore publishes no direct result, Denmark's direct page is archived and stale, and Brazil's current waiver covers diplomatic, official and service passports rather than ordinary passports. None of the 30 fallback `visa_on_arrival` rows is therefore verified or positively contradicted.

Djibouti's current e-government material proves that an approved eVisa is delivered electronically and replaces the visa formerly issued at an entry point as a general process. Law 40/AN/19, however, preserves bilateral and special regimes and assigns the eVisa channel to applicants coming from countries where Djibouti lacks a diplomatic or consular mission. That operand is the country of application, not passport nationality, and neither the law nor the portal publishes the missing nationality eligibility roster. Singapore's fallback waiver and the other 29 fallback arrival visas all remain unverified; generic portal availability is not used to silently relabel them as eVisas.

South Sudan's live Ministry of Interior immigration portal authenticates the electronic workflow: online application and payment, approval, a downloadable PDF before travel, verification at entry and subsequent passport stamping. Its public page nevertheless asks whether the traveller is eligible without publishing a nationality list or a result for any queued ordinary passport. The process proves issuance taxonomy but cannot establish the missing nationality operand, so all 30 fallback `evisa` rows remain visibly uncovered. The Foreign Ministry host also failed normal certificate verification during review and was excluded rather than being treated as current evidence.

Three exact, independently reviewed audits partition another 85 unsupported relationships: 25 Balkan-passport rows across five French overseas destinations, 30 Kosovo-passport outbound rows and 30 South Sudan-passport outbound rows. The current French territorial instruments do contain short-stay exemptions for Albanian, Montenegrin, North Macedonian, Serbian and Ukrainian travellers, but the relevant exemption is expressly limited to biometric passports. Serbia is further split by an exclusion for passports issued by its Coordination Directorate. Ukraine's extended, replacement and child travel documents add another material document distinction. MultiPass currently stores a bare ordinary-passport nationality code and cannot state that every valid ordinary document satisfies those predicates. All 25 rows therefore remain uncovered rather than flattening a conditional exemption into a nationality-wide result. The instruments also confirm that Schengen and metropolitan-France treatment cannot be copied to an overseas territory.

The Kosovo audit replays current official schedules and portals for 30 destinations that were otherwise close to complete. The sources either do not identify a Kosovo ordinary passport or expose only an incomplete conditional route. Sri Lanka, for example, expressly allows a Kosovo applicant to apply through a local sponsor at the Department head office, but does not state the resulting pre-travel issuance outcome or reconcile that route with ETA. The South Sudan outbound audit likewise adds no relationship. ## Pass 330 result: current biometric-passport baseline for three Balkan issuers

Three exact issuer-side audits revisit 15 of Pass 329's document-qualified French-overseas holds: Albanian, Montenegrin and North Macedonian ordinary passports into French Guiana, French Polynesia, Mayotte, New Caledonia and Réunion. Independent authors and reviewers established a complete current-document chain for each issuer, so all 15 relationships are now verified as `visa_free`. The territorial rules still require a biometric passport; the new evidence establishes that every currently valid standard ordinary passport in these three nationality rows satisfies that predicate rather than deleting it.

Albania's current consolidated passport law has required biometric security data since 2010 and caps ordinary-passport validity at ten years for applicants over 16 and five years for younger applicants. The recorded child-data correction and 2020–21 extension expired years ago, while the 2025 extension applied only to voting and ended on 31 May 2025. Montenegro's current consolidated law defines its standard passport as an electronic document whose chip contains biometric facial and fingerprint data; former-FRY passports expired on 31 December 2009, and the distinct return certificate is excluded. North Macedonia's current issuer guidance caps ordinary validity at ten years, the current official civil-document record says all passports have been biometric since April 2012, and pre-name-change passports ceased international validity in February 2024. Its under-12 fingerprint-enrolment exception does not create a separate non-biometric ordinary-passport class.

The policies retain each current French territorial limit: French Guiana, Réunion and Mayotte allow up to 90 days in any 180-day period, while French Polynesia and New Caledonia allow three months in any six-month period. Work, residence, transit, special documents and final admission remain separate. Serbia stays unresolved because Coordination Directorate passports are expressly excluded; Ukraine stays unresolved because currently relevant extended, replacement and child documents preserve a material document split.

## Pass 331 result: Chad eVisa supersession and French West Indies biometric intersection

Chad's official 11 May 2026 notice makes evisa.td the exclusive channel for every new non-diplomatic and non-courtesy visa request. The live service confirms that the entry visa is issued online, and the June circular supplies the pre-entry processing route. Seven European ordinary-passport rows (ES, SE, BE, FR, GB, NL and AT) and six further rows (DK, AU, MX, SC, RU and KW) are therefore corrected from advance `visa_required` to `evisa`, effective 11 May 2026. Visas regularly issued before the 21 May cut-over remain valid until expiry; later outside-platform visas do not permit entry. PS to TD remains unresolved because the retained State of Palestine ordinary-passport schedule fails TLS validation on replay, and the generic Chadian non-exempt wording cannot independently prove Palestinian recognition or document scope.

French West Indies adds three current `visa_free` rows for Albanian, Montenegrin and North Macedonian standard ordinary biometric passports. This is a four-territory intersection—Guadeloupe, Martinique, Saint-Martin and Saint-Barthélemy—not a transfer from metropolitan France, Schengen or another overseas territory. Both current Légifrance Annex-II rule families separately impose and satisfy the biometric-passport waiver for all three issuers. The shared visitor limit is 90 days in any 180-day period; work, residence, special documents and final admission remain separate.

The bundled fallback was synchronized on 25 August 2026. That refresh also changed TR to PS from `visa_free` to `visa_required`, but the route-aware official audit still cannot normalize one destination-wide status: Palestine itself states no visa for Turkish citizens, while Israel-controlled access introduces a third-country visa condition. The refreshed row therefore remains visibly unverified rather than being silently accepted. Regenerated current coverage is 40,030 of 44,974 relationships overall: Asia 5,467; Europe 10,058; Africa 8,904; Oceania 3,962; Caribbean 4,736; Americas 4,878; Middle East 2,025. Canonical evidence now contains 826 reviewed batches, 3,470 official source records and 1,948 policies.

## Pass 332 result: Comoros bilateral current-force audit

One exact, independently reviewed audit reopens the two unsupported Mauritian and Kenyan ordinary-passport relationships into Comoros. Neither row is promoted. Complete-matrix evidence remains 40,030 of 44,974 relationships (89.0%), with 4,944 not covered and zero old or stale rows as of 25 August 2026. No regional total, displayed access label, mobility score or rank changes. Canonical evidence now contains 827 reviewed batches, 3,474 official source records and 1,948 policies.

For Mauritius, the official records disagree. The Comoros Presidency announced a visa exemption on 27 April 2017, while Mauritius Foreign Affairs' countrywise table updated 17 November 2025 directs Mauritian travellers to obtain a USD 100 visa at a Comorian airport. The older announcement has no recovered implementation or current-force confirmation, and the newer passport-government table is expressly informational and lacks current destination-side corroboration. The fallback `visa_on_arrival` result is therefore plausible but remains visibly unverified.

For Kenya, the official 6 July 2023 Comoros–Kenya joint communique says Comoros removed the visa requirement immediately for every holder of a valid Kenya-issued travel document; the Comorian President's speech independently says Kenyans may enter without visas. That is a strong historical correction lead, but no current Comorian immigration schedule, gazetted implementation, later official reaffirmation or repeal was recovered. Applying the same current-force rule used for other dated schedules, the audit does not relabel the fallback `visa_on_arrival` row as `visa_free`. Both records remain retained for a future recheck rather than being presented as current proof.

## Pass 333 result: United Kingdom Standard Visitor eVisa cutover

The United Kingdom's complete digital-issuance cutover corrects all 114 ordinary-passport Visa National List relationships from traditional `visa_required` to `evisa`, effective 1 July 2026. The nationality liability and individual approval burden did not disappear: applicants still apply before travel, pay the fee, provide supporting documents and normally attend a visa application centre for biometrics. What changed is the issued permission. UKVI stopped every new visa vignette on 1 July and now gives successful applicants a digital record of their visa and conditions through a UKVI account.

The review deliberately does not backdate the complete cohort to 25 February 2026. The ministerial announcement for that phase said only that most visa-national visitor applicants would receive eVisas and did not publish an exhaustive nationality or application-location split. The older policy therefore ends conservatively on 30 June; physical vignettes already issued remain valid until their individual expiry. A Form for Accompanying an eVisa, issued where the Home Office does not accept the passport or travel document, supplements the linked eVisa and does not create a continuing vignette route.

This was a material accuracy correction but not a mobility-score correction. MultiPass scores neither eVisas nor traditional visas because both require approval before departure, so all passport scores, ranks and combination results remain unchanged. Current-status evidence also remains 40,030 of 44,974 relationships (89.0%), with 4,944 pending and zero old or stale rows as of 25 August 2026. The bundled fallback changes exactly the 114 UK cells and no others. Canonical evidence now contains 828 reviewed batches, 3,478 official source records and 1,949 policies.

## Pass 334 result: South Africa's locally branded ETA platform

South Africa's current digital route for Chinese, Indian, Indonesian and Mexican ordinary-passport short visitors is independently confirmed and remains correctly normalized as `evisa`. Although the service is branded Electronic Travel Authorisation, the current Home Affairs plan describes automated digital visa adjudication, the responsible minister calls these the four visa-required countries in the pilot, and current DIRCO guidance requires all four nationalities to apply online for a short-term visitor visa. An approved applicant receives the permission digitally before travel. Under MultiPass's outcome-based taxonomy this is an electronically issued visa, not advance permission under a visa exemption.

The dedicated current policy replaces those four codes inside the older 27-country eVisa group without changing their displayed result. The other 23 passports remain supported by the existing DHA eVisa route. The reviewed conditions preserve the ordinary-passport and short-visitor scope, pre-travel application and approval, passport scan, live facial image, payment, supported-airport and border-verification requirements. The August launch says additional visa-required countries will be added progressively; no prospective nationality is treated as already eligible. Visa-exempt travellers' optional use of the platform likewise does not replace their underlying visa-free status.

Independent review rejected a proposed `eta` reclassification before promotion. That mistake would have added one mobility point each to China, India, Indonesia and Mexico even though the four travellers still need an individually adjudicated visitor visa before departure. No fallback label, score, rank, comparison or coverage total therefore changes. Complete-matrix evidence remains 40,030 of 44,974 relationships (89.0%), with 4,944 pending and zero old or stale rows as of 25 August 2026. Canonical evidence now contains 829 reviewed batches, 3,482 official source records and 1,950 policies.

Pass selection also replayed the full 137-row North Macedonia, 116-row Saudi Arabia and 84-row Ukraine residual columns without finding a safe addition. ## Pass 335 result: Singapore's current visa-exemption Order

Singapore's current S 441/2026 Order adds a direct statutory evidence policy to the Chinese ordinary-passport relationship. The Order commenced on 1 July 2026, expressly revoked the former O 4 Order and exempts a non-citizen holding a valid ordinary passport issued by the People's Republic of China, subject to its multiple-entry-pass carve-out. The current instrument states no visitor-stay duration, so the policy does not transplant the predecessor Order's duration language; checkpoint admission and the electronic Visit Pass period remain separate.

The rest of S 441/2026 is not treated as an ordinary-passport nationality schedule. Its permanent-resident, head-of-state, UN laissez-passer, visiting-force, crew, accredited-mission, diplomatic, official, service, public-affairs and emergency provisions remain outside the catalog's standard ordinary-passport visitor rows. The Order's chapeau also preserves countries approved separately under Immigration Act section 9B(2), so it cannot by itself generate a global visa-free complement.

Independent replay of ICA's current 25 June visa-required list, MFA's current electronic-issuance instructions and the Singapore High Commission's explicit operational complement found no status correction. Complete-matrix evidence therefore remains 40,030 of 44,974 relationships (89.0%), with 4,944 pending and zero old or stale rows as of 25 August 2026. ## Pass 341 result: reviewed unknown relationships

The runtime model now distinguishes a reviewed unresolved relationship from an unreviewed imported hypothesis. A `reviewed unknown` override is exact-pair metadata, not a visa policy: it stores the rejected fallback category, official source IDs, reason, review date and mandatory recheck date. It renders the existing `unknown` access status, remains pending and red in evidence coverage, stays excluded from the sitemap and search indexing, and links to a source-backed detail page. It must never be inferred merely from absence in a general list.

Six relationships pass that stricter gate. Azerbaijan's official ASAN conditions limit eVisa issuance to its Foreign Ministry-approved list, and the closed current list excludes Kosovo; XK→AZ changes from `evisa` to `unknown`. Türkiye's current Foreign Ministry register materially splits Algerian, Egyptian, Iraqi and Libyan ordinary-passport treatment by age and, for some groups, qualifying third-country documents; DZ→TR, EG→TR, IQ→TR and LY→TR change from an inaccurate passport-wide `evisa` label to `unknown`. Spain's current Foreign Ministry guidance says Iran's border visa-on-arrival route has not officially resumed and requires pre-application without establishing whether the resulting permission is electronic or mission-issued; ES→IR changes from `visa_on_arrival` to `unknown`.

The first five changes do not affect mobility scoring because eVisas were already unscored. Spain loses one mobility point, from 191 to 190 in the refreshed fallback, and moves from dense rank 3 to rank 4. Canonical evidence remains 40,030 of 44,974 relationships (89.0%) with 4,944 pending: reviewed unknowns improve displayed accuracy without claiming that a replacement route has been verified. Canonical policy and source totals remain 831 batches, 3,487 sources and 1,953 policies.

Six exact ordinary-passport relationships now have complete current source chains. ## Pass 356 result: remaining home-citizenship relationships

Twenty-eight diagonal passport-to-home-destination relationships now have direct current official support: Democratic Republic of the Congo, Côte d’Ivoire, Eswatini, Mauritius, Nigeria, Rwanda, South Africa, Uganda, Costa Rica, El Salvador, Honduras, Panama, Dominican Republic, The Bahamas, Afghanistan, Brunei, China, Hong Kong, Macao, Mongolia, Myanmar, Pakistan, Monaco, Montenegro, Oman, Papua New Guinea, Samoa and Solomon Islands. The sources are current constitutions, passport or immigration laws, official gazettes and competent passport, citizenship or border-authority guidance. We did not promote a cell merely because its passport and destination codes match.

`citizenship` is a narrow catalog identity classification. It means that the ordinary national passport belongs to the destination's own citizen, national or right-of-abode class, or that the authority directly establishes that class's return or entry basis. It does not claim immunity from passport validity, identity, port-of-entry, public-health, criminal, emergency, national-security or other lawful controls. HKSAR and Macao SAR passports are similarly limited to the official citizenship and permanent-resident predicates for those specific documents.

Forty-one diagonals remain pending after official-source review: Angola, Botswana, Burkina Faso, Burundi, Cameroon, Central African Republic, Chad, Egypt, Equatorial Guinea, Eritrea, Gabon, Guinea, Guinea-Bissau, Lesotho, Mali, Mauritania, Morocco, Mozambique, Niger, São Tomé and Príncipe, Senegal, Sierra Leone, South Sudan, Tunisia, Belize, Guyana, Venezuela, Barbados, Cuba, Dominica, Grenada, Saint Lucia, Saint Vincent and the Grenadines, Cambodia, Kyrgyzstan, Laos, Nepal, North Korea, Palestinian Territory, Syria and Yemen. Existing exact partitions for São Tomé and Príncipe (135 rows), Mali (157), Niger (158) and Guinea-Bissau (159) were rechecked against current official sources. São Tomé's eVisa service remains a workflow without a nationality schedule. Mali's generic required result has a known invalid-input default and unresolved regional exceptions. Niger publishes no nationality-complete schedule, and Guinea-Bissau's unauthenticated legacy visa host is stale and contaminated with unrelated content. These five destination audits cover 754 exact holds without adding a policy.

## Pass 370 result: Côte d'Ivoire and named-schedule replay

Côte d'Ivoire now has an exact current 133-row residual audit. Current SNEDAI and Foreign Ministry pages replay normally and establish physical airport delivery after electronic pre-enrolment and approval, but do not publish an exhaustive eligibility complement for the omitted passports. No residual relationship is promoted from workflow availability alone.

Existing exact current partitions for North Macedonia (137 rows), Tunisia (153) and Liberia (159) were also replayed. North Macedonia's selector supplies named results but no authenticated exhaustive complement. Tunisia's eVisa remains a future implementation—the current procurement result says the platform tender was unsuccessful—and E-Consulat names no nationality schedule. Liberia's generic online and consular forms likewise do not establish nationality-complete issuance. The four audits cover 582 exact holds and leave coverage unchanged.

## Pass 371 result: remaining high-residual partition check

Exact current candidates were confirmed for Comoros and Iraq (166 rows each), Djibouti and South Sudan (168 each), Turkmenistan (170), Sudan and Syria (174 each), the Central African Republic (175), North Korea (185) and Afghanistan (187). Together they account for 1,733 current holds. The Palestinian Territory is the remaining high-residual destination without a single exact current inbound partition. Its 198 rows depend on non-representable West Bank/Gaza, Israeli entry-document, registry, crossing and permit distinctions. Existing outbound and relationship-specific audits must not be presented as an inbound destination-wide completion. This is an explicit research gap, not a verified visa-required complement.

## Pass 372 result: low-gap Western passport replay

Nine Western passport ETA hypotheses into the Palestinian Territory remain pending. Current official guidance directly establishes ETA-IL for Israeli-controlled entry, and Ireland expressly discusses the rule while crossing into Palestine, but that does not normalize a destination-wide Palestinian baseline. West Bank entry depends on route and Israeli control, while Gaza has materially different permit and closure conditions. France, Germany, the United Kingdom, Italy, Poland, Spain, the United States, Canada and Ireland therefore remain exact holds rather than receiving an overbroad `eta` policy.

Eleven Afghanistan and North Korea hypotheses also remain pending. Current British, Canadian, Italian, Irish, German, Spanish and United States guidance frequently establishes visa liability, but does not establish an operational accepted issuer for the relevant ordinary visitor. The strongest initial proposal—United States-to-Afghanistan as advance `visa_required`—was rejected on independent review. The U.S. advisory says a visa is required before travel but also warns that Republic-era mission visas may be refused unless issued by authorities aligned with the de facto government; no cited current Afghan issuer is shown as available to ordinary U.S. tourist-passport applicants. That imported label is retained as an explicit evidentiary conflict, not promoted.

Pass 372 records 20 exact holds and one current operational-route conflict. Complete-matrix evidence remains 40,103 of 44,974 foreign relationships (89.2%), with 4,871 pending and zero old or stale rows. Canonical evidence remains at 849 reviewed batches, 3,569 official source records and 2,007 policies.

## Pass 373 result: Saudi current-route partition and schedule recovery

Saudi Arabia adds 114 current relationships from its live Tourism Authority nationality and selection-specific route APIs. One hundred and eight passports in the service's `noneligible` group receive the ordinary advance `visa_required` baseline: selecting `OTHER` directs those travellers to an Embassy, Consulate or Tasheer centre and publishes the advance application requirements. That result does not erase the separate routes for travellers who independently qualify through a valid United States, United Kingdom or Schengen visa or residence permit, GCC residence, pilgrimage or stopover products.

Indonesia, India, Jordan, Egypt, Bangladesh and Pakistan change from the imported `visa_required` label to conditional package `evisa`. The live selector assigns each passport to `Visa by Package`, and the matching `OTHER` response expressly offers an instant tourist eVisa through a qualified integrated package. A licensed four-star hotel, round-trip flight, online visa and insurance, package-value and composition rules, and advance purchase remain material conditions. This is not treated as visa-free or arrival-issued access. The six corrections do not affect mobility scores or ranks because eVisa and advance visa are both outside the scored categories.

Israel and the Republic of the Congo remain unresolved. The current country response omits Israel, while its one Congo record uses Democratic Republic of the Congo flag material and provides no reproducible Republic of the Congo crosswalk. The retained Israeli arrival label is therefore still an unverified fallback, not current-status evidence. Saudi Arabia now has verified evidence for 196 of 198 foreign passport relationships.

Two parallel source-recovery audits add no speculative policy. The British Virgin Islands' recovered S.I. 26/2026 exempts only Territory-born children under age 11 with resident parents; it is not a foreign visitor nationality schedule, so all 96 scoped rows remain held. Trinidad and Tobago's current instructions and FAQ prove that approved entry visas are electronic and delivered by email, but the positive-only waiver list and authentication-blocked eligibility service do not publish a closed nationality complement. All 107 scoped rows remain held.

Complete-matrix evidence rises to 40,217 of 44,974 foreign relationships (89.4%), leaving 4,757 pending and zero old or stale rows as of 26 August 2026. Canonical evidence now contains 850 reviewed batches, 3,569 official source records and 2,009 policies.

## Pass 374 result: final Saudi relationship adjudication

Saudi Arabia's two remaining foreign-passport relationships receive exact independent audits but remain pending. The current closed eVisa terms exclude Israel, while the live nationality API omits it; neither negative result establishes an Israeli visa on arrival, a consular route, passport recognition or destination-side refusal. Israel's current government guidance says a visa is required and separately prohibits citizen travel to Saudi Arabia, but that person-level outbound restriction is not proof of Saudi admission and is not normalized as `entry_restricted`.

The Republic of the Congo cannot safely inherit the generic `noneligible` advance route. Visit Saudi currently returns separate `Congo` and `Democratic Republic of the Congo` labels, but both reuse Democratic Republic of the Congo flag material and the frontend identifies selections by mutable country name rather than ISO or another stable sovereign-country key. The route configuration proves what a correctly mapped noneligible selection receives; it does not prove which Congo record is CG. Current Saudi visa terms and Foreign Ministry material supply no reproducible crosswalk.

No policy, status, score, rank or coverage total changes. The Saudi destination remains at 196 of 198 verified foreign relationships, with the final two gaps now captured in exact current candidates rather than only a bulk residual audit.

## Pass 376 result: Angola's preauthorised border-visa route

Angola adds 88 current ordinary-passport relationships from a jointly closed legal and operational route. Decree 189/23 Article 4 permits the tourist application online or through an Angolan mission, requires a decision within three working days, sends a pre-authorisation before travel, and then affixes the visa to the travel document only after confirmation at the frontier. The current SME page independently names each of the 88 passports in its country selector and routes every selection to the same tourist product. This is therefore normalized as conditional `visa_on_arrival`, not eVisa: the approval is electronic, but the visa itself is issued at the border.

The route is tourism-only and requires advance approval, a passport valid for more than six months, a return ticket and a vaccination certificate when applicable; frontier confirmation and final admission remain separate. Eighty-four imported `visa_required` rows and four imported `evisa` rows—Andorra, Cuba, Guinea-Bissau and Venezuela—change to `visa_on_arrival`. Each affected passport gains one mobility point, and dense ranks are recomputed from the corrected scores.

Four relationships remain pending. South Sudan and Kosovo are absent from the live selector; Sudan cannot be transferred to South Sudan, and no generic product statement proves acceptance of an unmapped document. São Tomé and Príncipe and Zambia are selectable, but Decree 189/23 expressly preserves treaty regimes. Current official waiver and facilitation records do not establish one controlling category for either imported `visa_free` row, so the easier labels are neither endorsed nor overwritten by the conditional border route.

Parallel exact rechecks add no speculative policy. Panama's current named visa-free, authorised-visa and stamped-visa cohorts omit Macao and Timor-Leste and name Sudan rather than South Sudan, without an express residual complement. Nigeria's current F5A material proves electronic delivery for an eligible applicant but does not expose a reproducible nationality entitlement for Georgia, Eswatini or Afghanistan. Those six rows remain held.

Complete-matrix evidence rises to 40,305 of 44,974 foreign relationships (89.6%), leaving 4,669 pending and zero old or stale rows as of 26 August 2026. Angola now has verified evidence for 194 of 198 foreign passports. Canonical evidence contains 851 reviewed batches, 3,574 official source records and 2,010 policies.

Complete-matrix evidence rises to 40,342 of 44,974 foreign relationships (89.7%), leaving 4,632 pending and zero old or stale rows as of 26 August 2026. Canonical evidence contains 853 reviewed batches, 3,584 official source records and 2,012 policies.

## Residual audit coverage milestone

Every one of the 4,632 relationships that remains unverified in the public matrix now appears in at least one explicit candidate audit as a supported hypothesis, documented conflict or exact unresolved hold. The repository contains 977 candidate files, giving 100% research-audit coverage of the current pending set. This is not presented as 100% verified evidence: the public status remains 89.7% because a reviewed inability to establish a route is different from proof of the route itself.

Run `npm run evidence:audit-coverage` to recompute the invariant. It scans the current corrected access snapshot, canonical evidence and every candidate partition, fails when a pending relationship has no audit record, and is now part of CI and the full deployment gate. Future data refreshes therefore cannot silently create an unaudited red cell.

## Official bulk sources identified for later passes

- [Hong Kong Immigration Department inbound visa-requirement CSV](https://www.immd.gov.hk/opendata/eng/law-and-security/visas/visit_visa_entry_permit_requirements_HKSAR.csv), published through the [Hong Kong government data catalog](https://data.gov.hk/en-data/dataset/hk-immd-set4-visit-visa-entry-permit-requirements-hksar/resource/7721f67e-80e9-4306-b379-66a47c6a617a). It distinguishes ordinary, biometric, diplomatic, official, and special British documents, so normalization must preserve those exceptions rather than reducing every row mechanically.
- [Hong Kong Immigration Department outbound visa-free dataset specification](https://www.immd.gov.hk/opendata/eng/law-and-security/personal_documentation/data_specification_for_VFA_list.pdf). This can validate access reported for HKSAR passports, subject to the published document-type fields.
- [Council of the European Union visa-agreement overview](https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/infographics/eu-visa-agreements-with-non-eu-countries/) and individual EUR-Lex agreements. Treat the overview as discovery and cite the underlying agreement for scope and effective dates.

These are candidates, not silently imported truth. Their country naming, passport types, exceptions, and update semantics need a reviewed mapping before they can change the access snapshot.

## Prompt for later research passes

Use this prompt with a research-capable model. A human or stronger review model must still open every cited URL before merge.

> Research official visa-access evidence for the assigned destination or agreement. Treat the supplied MultiPass Rank statuses as unverified hypotheses. Cite only primary government, official gazette, treaty repository, government open-data, immigration, border, foreign-ministry, embassy, or consulate sources. Do not cite commercial indexes, Timatic summaries, Wikipedia, airlines, agents, blogs, or search snippets. For every supported rule return: destination ISO code; affected passport ISO codes or explicit exclusions; normalized status (`visa_free`, `eta`, `visa_on_arrival`, `evisa`, `visa_required`, `entry_restricted`, or `citizenship`); effective and end dates only when stated; summary; material conditions; source title, direct URL, publisher, jurisdiction, language, type, and review date; official application URL and steps if relevant; confidence; and conflicts with the supplied status. Use `entry_restricted` only for a current official prohibition or suspension of ordinary visitor entry, and preserve every grandfathering rule and exception. Quote no more than 25 words from a source for internal review and do not publish the quotation. Say `not established` instead of guessing.

For a lower-capability model, use the stricter packet, schema, validation, and review workflow in [visa-evidence-model-handoff.md](visa-evidence-model-handoff.md). Candidate output must never be merged into canonical evidence without a stronger reviewer opening every cited source.

## Review checklist

- Every citation opens on an official domain and directly supports the claim.
- Dates distinguish announcement, publication, and legal effect.
- Tourist access is not confused with work, residence, transit, diplomatic-passport, or airport-visa rules.
- The policy’s passport list matches the official document exactly.
- Application instructions do not promise admission or processing outcomes.
- Unsupported pairs remain honest placeholders and are not added to the sitemap.
- The source’s terms are respected; store factual metadata and links rather than republishing documents.
